<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every prime <i>p</i> ≥ 5, the canonical map $`L_0L_{K(3)}S\to L_0L_{K(2)}L_{K(3)}S`$ for the sphere spectrum <i>S</i> is nonzero on <i>π</i><sub>−3</sub>. Consequently, the height-three strong chromatic splitting formula is false in this range, even as an equivalence of underlying $`E(2)`$-local spectra without specified summand maps.

- **claim-main-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every prime $p\geq5$, the canonical map $$\label{eq:main-map} \tau_{L_{K(3)}S}:L_0L_{K(3)}S\longrightarrow L_0L_{K(2)}L_{K(3)}S$$ is nonzero on $\pi_{-3}$.
  - Explanation: For every prime at least five, the canonical comparison from the rationalised height-three Morava-local sphere to the rationalised height-two localisation of it does not vanish in homotopy degree minus three.
- **claim-strong-splitting-false** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-strong)): For every prime $p\geq5$, there is no equivalence of $E(2)$-local spectra $L_2L_{K(3)}S\simeq\mathcal W_3$, with $\mathcal W_3$ as in <a href="#eq:proposed-wedge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:proposed-wedge">[eq:proposed-wedge]</a>. Thus the height-three strong chromatic splitting formula is false in this range, whether or not an equivalence is required to preserve its specified unit summand.
  - Explanation: The proposed wedge of localised spheres cannot be equivalent to the height-two localisation of the height-three local sphere, even without any condition on summand maps, so the strong splitting formula fails.
- **claim-solid-resolutions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-solid-resolutions)): Let $G$ be a compact $p$-adic analytic group without elements of order $p$, and let $\Lambda_G=\mathbb Z_p[[G]]$. There is a finite resolution $P_\bullet\to\mathbb Z_p$ by finitely generated projective profinite $\Lambda_G$-modules. Its length can be taken to be $\dim G$. For any derived-solid $\mathbb Z_p$-complex $K$ with a continuous $G$-action, the point-valued derived invariants are computed by $$\label{eq:finite-resolution} R\Gamma(G,K)(*)\simeq \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\Lambda_G}(P_\bullet,K).$$ Here the right side means the finite total complex of morphisms in solid modules. In particular, invariants of a module in the heart have cohomology only in degrees $0,\ldots,\dim G$.* *Suppose $K$ is bounded below and an operator $\gamma$ commutes with $G$. If $\gamma$ acts by $\lambda_j\in\mathbb Q_p$ on $H^j(K)(*)[1/p]$, then it acts by the same scalar on every term in the $j$th row of the rationalized descent spectral sequence $$H^s\bigl(G,H^j(K)\bigr)(*)[1/p] \ \Longrightarrow\ H^{s+j}\bigl(R\Gamma(G,K)(*)\bigr)[1/p].$$ For $K=\mathscr C_X$, the abutment is $H^{s+j}_{\mathrm b}([X/G])$.
  - Explanation: A compact p-adic analytic group without p-torsion has a finite projective resolution of the trivial module over its completed group ring. This computes derived invariants of solid coefficients by a finite complex and shows that commuting scalar operators act row by row in the descent spectral sequence.
- **claim-group-classes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-group-classes)): For $G=P_n$ or $J_n$, where $n=2,3$, $$H^1_{\mathrm b}(G)=\mathbb Q_p, \qquad H^2_{\mathrm b}(G)=0, \qquad H^3_{\mathrm b}(G)=\mathbb Q_p.$$ For $n=2,3$ and every compact open subgroup $K\subset J_n$, restriction gives an isomorphism in every degree, with the compact comparison identifying its target: $$H^*_{\mathrm b}(J_n)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{res}}H^*_{\mathrm b}(K) \cong H^*(\mathfrak{gl}_n(\mathbb Q_p),\mathbb Q_p).$$ In degree three the block inclusion $J_2% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21AA}% \hookrightarrow\EndAccSupp{}J_3$ induces a nonzero map. Fix arbitrary nonzero classes $$e_{P_n}\in H^3_{\mathrm b}(P_n),\qquad e_{J_n}\in H^3_{\mathrm b}(J_n).$$
  - Explanation: The division-algebra stabiliser and the determinant-one linear group have the same low-degree rational cohomology: one-dimensional in degrees one and three and zero in degree two. Restriction to compact open subgroups is an isomorphism, and the block inclusion is nonzero in degree three.
- **claim-parabolic-restriction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-parabolic)): Inflation induces an isomorphism $$H^*_{\mathrm b}(\mathbb Z_p^\times\times J_2) \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H^*_{\mathrm b}(D).$$ There is a scalar $b\in\mathbb Q_p^\times$ such that $$\rho_3^*e_{J_3}=b\,\rho_2^*e_{J_2}.$$
  - Explanation: Inflation from the Levi quotient is an isomorphism on cohomology of the parabolic subgroup, because the unipotent part is acyclic. Hence the rank-three primitive class restricts to a nonzero multiple of the rank-two class.
- **claim-drinfeld-integral-cohomology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-drinfeld)): For $0\leq j\leq d$ there are compatible $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1}(\mathbb Q_p)\times\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(\overline\mathbb Q_p/\mathbb Q_p)$-equivariant isomorphisms of groups $$H^j_{\mathrm{int}}(\Omega_C^d)(j) \simeq\operatorname{Sp}_j(\mathbb Z_p)^*, \qquad H^j_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(\Omega_C^d,\mathbb F_p(j)) \simeq\operatorname{Sp}_j(\mathbb F_p)^*.$$ Here $\operatorname{Sp}_j$ is the generalized Steinberg representation of (Colmez, Dospinescu, and Nizioł 2021, sec. 4.1) [[CDN2021](#ref-CDN2021)], the star denotes its continuous coefficient-linear dual with weak topology, and the right sides have trivial Galois action. These groups vanish for $j>d$, and $H^0_{\mathrm{int}}(\Omega_C^d)=\mathbb Z_p$ by constants. In particular, on the untwisted integral group in degree $j$, an arithmetic Galois element $\gamma$ acts by $\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(\gamma)^{-j}$.
  - Explanation: Integral and mod p cohomology of Drinfeld space are identified, equivariantly for the linear group and Galois, with duals of generalised Steinberg representations. Galois acts on the untwisted integral group in degree j through a power of the cyclotomic character.
- **claim-completed-tower** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-tower)): The field $\widehat L$ is perfectoid of characteristic $p$, and is isomorphic, as a valued field over $k$, to the completed perfection of a Laurent-series field: $$\widehat L\simeq \left(\bigcup_{r\ge0}k((z^{1/p^r}))\right)^{\wedge}.$$ There is a $G$-equivariant identification of v-sheaves over $\mathop{\mathrm{Spd}}k$ $$\mathop{\mathrm{Spd}}\widehat L\simeq \operatorname{Surj}(\mathcal E_3^0,\mathcal E_2^0).$$ Consequently $$Y=[\mathop{\mathrm{Spd}}\widehat L/G]$$ classifies surjections $\mathcal E_3\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal E_2$ between $P_n$-structured forms. In particular $Y$ carries the universal surjection used below.* *Let $S_3=\ker N_3$ and $T_3=P_3/S_3=\mathbb Z_p^{\times}$. Then $$[\mathop{\mathrm{Spd}}\widehat L/S_3] \simeq\operatorname{BC}(-1/2)^*$$ as v-sheaves, with the following residual $P_2\times T_3$-action. On extension classes in $\operatorname{Ext}^1(\mathcal E_2^0,\mathcal O)$, write $\lambda_*$ for pushout by $\lambda$ on $\mathcal O$ and $b^*$ for pullback by $b$ on $\mathcal E_2^0$. For the framing action $f% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}% \mapsto\EndAccSupp{}bfg^{-1}$ and $a=N_3(g)$, the action is $$(b,a)\cdot\xi =\bigl(aN_2(b)^{-1}\bigr)_*(b^{-1})^*\xi.$$ In particular, the $T_3$-action is pushout by $a$ on the kernel. Only this restriction of the action on the extension chart is used below.
  - Explanation: The completed Gross–Torii field is a perfectoid field of characteristic p given by a completed perfection of a Laurent-series field. Its spectrum classifies surjections between the two basic bundles, and the quotient by the norm-kernel has an explicit residual action.
- **claim-constant-acyclicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-constant-acyclicity)): The actual maps of constants induce equivalences $$\begin{aligned} k&\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }R\Gamma(P_3^1,\widehat L), \label{eq:finite-constant-map}\\ C^{\flat}&\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } R\Gamma_v(X_C,\mathcal O^{\flat}). \label{eq:geometric-constant-map} \end{aligned}$$ These are equivalences of derived solid algebras with residual $H$-action. The $H$-action on the left sides is trivial. The maps are natural for the change of constants $k\to C^{\flat}$ and for the arithmetic actions preserving the chosen base data.
  - Explanation: After taking invariants under the norm-unit subgroup, the maps from constants are equivalences, both for the finite field and for the geometric constants. The top cohomology line carries a nontrivial character of the finite cyclic quotient and is killed by averaging.
- **claim-completed-theory** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-completed-theory)): There are continuous $G$-equivariant maps of solid $E_\infty$-rings $$\label{eq:completed-map} E_3^{\square}\longrightarrow\widehat B^{\square} \longleftarrow E_2^{\square},$$ where $G$ acts through $P_3$ on the first source and through $P_2$ on the second. The right arrow is canonical connected base change. The target is even periodic, with $$\pi_0\widehat B^{\square}(*)\simeq W(\widehat L)[[u'_1]].$$ Invariantly, its coefficients are complete base changes of the height-two deformation ring and its invertible even coefficient lines. The natural map $W(\widehat L)\to\pi_0\widehat B^{\square}$ is $G$-equivariant. Every profinite evaluation $\widehat B^{\square}(T)$ is an ordinary $K(2)$-local spectrum.* *The second map in <a href="#eq:completed-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:completed-map">[eq:completed-map]</a> induces an $H$-equivariant equivalence $$E_2^{\square}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } (\widehat B^{\square})^{hP_3^1},$$ where $\overline Z$ acts trivially on the source. Moreover, for every $t\in\mathbb Z$ it induces an equivalence on the individual coefficient complexes $$\pi_tE_2^{\square}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } R\Gamma(P_3^1,\pi_t\widehat B^{\square}).$$ In particular, for $s\ge0$ and all $t$ there are natural isomorphisms $$\label{eq:coefficient-descent} H^s(H,\pi_tE_2^{\square}) \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } H^s(G,\pi_t\widehat B^{\square}).$$ These comparisons preserve the coefficient maps from Witt constants.* *Finally, put $\Delta=\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(k/\mathbb F_p)$ and $G_n=P_n\rtimes\Delta$. The ordinary stabilizer-descent identification is $$\bigl((E_n^{\square})^{hG_n}\bigr)(*)\simeq L_{K(n)}S, \qquad n=2,3,$$ compatibly with the canonical maps to $E_n$.
  - Explanation: There is a completed even-periodic height-two Lubin–Tate theory receiving equivariant ring maps from both Morava E-theories. Taking fixed points of the first subgroup recovers the height-two theory coefficientwise, and the ordinary stabiliser descent recovers the local sphere.
- **claim-completed-oriented-map** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-completed-oriented-map)): Put $F=\widehat L(*)$, regarded here as the ordinary completed field. For a nonempty profinite set $T$, put $R_T=C_{\mathrm{cts}}(T,F)$ and let $\Gamma_T=\Gamma^{\mathrm{un}}_{3,R_T}$ be the full completed $p$-divisible group. Let $C_T$ be the base change of the connected term in the uniquely split sequence over $F$. It is identified with $(\Gamma_2)_{R_T}$ by the fixed tautological Gross–Torii formal isomorphism. There is a continuous adic $E_\infty$-map $$r_T:E(R_T,\Gamma_T)\longrightarrow E(R_T,C_T).$$ The maps are natural for profinite pullbacks and every continuous $G=P_3\times P_2$ family of pair maps. With the terminal value at the empty parameter set, pointwise application and hypersheafification give a continuous $G$-equivariant map of condensed $E_\infty$-rings $$r^{\mathrm{cond}}: E^{\mathrm{cond}}(\widehat L,\Gamma^{\mathrm{un}}_{3,\widehat L}) \longrightarrow E^{\mathrm{cond}}(\widehat L,C_{\widehat L}).$$* *Let $b_T:E_2\to E(R_T,\Gamma_T)$ be the pointwise ordinary $E_2$ tensor-factor map followed by completion, and let $c_T:E_2\to E(R_T,C_T)$ be the canonical connected map induced by $k\to R_T$. Then $r_Tb_T\simeq c_T$, naturally for the same parameters and pair families. This homotopy preserves the connected residue tag and orientation, and hence its coefficient and even-line maps. After condensation and solid localization, it gives $$r^{\mathrm{cond}}b^\square\simeq c^\square$$ as continuous $G$-equivariant maps of underlying solid spectra, where $b^\square$ is the tensor-factor map followed by completion and $c^\square$ is the canonical connected base-change map.
  - Explanation: Splitting the connected-étale sequence over the perfect completed field gives a section of oriented deformations and hence a retraction of the full completed theory onto the connected one. The retraction composed with the tensor-factor map is the canonical connected base change.
- **claim-completion-remark** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): The completion in <a href="#eq:completed-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:completed-map">[eq:completed-map]</a> is essential to the statements proved here. The uncompleted half-sphere equivalence is (Barthel et al. 2026, Conjectures 1.1.1 and 5.1.13) [[BMR2026](#ref-BMR2026)] [[BMR2026](#ref-BMR2026)]; (Barthel et al. 2026, Proposition 5.1.17) proves an implication from that conjecture to coheight-one splitting. The completed theorem above supplies an actual target for detection and does not assert an equivalence from the uncompleted fixed points to that target.
  - Explanation: The completed target gives an unconditional detection object; the uncompleted half-sphere statement remains conjectural and is not asserted.
- **claim-lower-modification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lower-modification)): Let $\mathcal E_n$ be a form of $\mathcal E_n^0$, and let $q:i_\infty^*\mathcal E_n\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal L$ be a quotient line. The elementary lower modification $$0\longrightarrow\mathcal V_n\longrightarrow\mathcal E_n \longrightarrow i_{\infty *}\mathcal L\longrightarrow0$$ is geometrically semistable of slope zero. It therefore defines a rank-$n$ rational local system.
  - Explanation: Modifying a basic degree-one bundle along a quotient line at the divisor produces a geometrically semistable bundle of slope zero, hence a rational local system.
- **claim-drinfeld-locus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-drinfeld-locus)): The upper modification $\mathcal E_\ell$ is semistable of slope $1/n$ if and only if $\ell$ lies in no proper $\mathbb Q_p$-rational subspace. Consequently its semistable locus is $$\Omega_C^{n-1} =\mathbb P_C^{n-1}\setminus \bigcup_{\substack{W\subset\mathbb Q_p^n\\ \dim W=n-1}}\mathbb P(W_C).$$ On this locus $\mathcal E_\ell$ is a form of $\mathcal E_n^0$.
  - Explanation: An upper modification of the trivial bundle is semistable exactly when its direction line avoids every proper rational subspace, so the semistable locus is Drinfeld space.
- **claim-two-realizations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-two-realizations)): Let $\mathcal M_n$ classify a form of $\mathcal E_n^0$ with $P_n$-structure and a quotient line at $\infty$. There are $G_F$-equivariant equivalences of v-stacks $$\label{eq:two-realizations} \mathcal M_n \simeq [\mathbb P(i_\infty^*\mathcal E_n^0)/P_n] \simeq [\mathcal Z_n/(P_n\times J_n)] \simeq [\Omega_C^{n-1}/J_n].$$ Its framing torsor defines $\mathcal M_n\to B_CP_n$, and its universal lower modification defines the indicated map $\mathcal M_n\to B_CJ_n$.
  - Explanation: The moduli stack of modifications has a projective-space presentation with the division-algebra group and a Drinfeld-space presentation with the determinant-lattice-preserving linear group, compatibly with Galois.
- **claim-filtered-modification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-filtered-modification)): The tautological quotient of $\mathcal E_2$ and its composite with $f$ give maps $m_n:\mathcal T\to\mathcal M_n$ and an exact sequence $$\label{eq:filtered-modification} 0\longrightarrow\ker(f)\longrightarrow\mathcal V_3 \longrightarrow\mathcal V_2\longrightarrow0 .$$ These are slope-zero bundles. Their corresponding rational local systems form an exact sequence of ranks $1,3,2$. With the induced determinant lattices this sequence is classified by a map $d:\mathcal T\to B_CD$, where $$D=\left\{ \begin{pmatrix}a&v\\0&A\end{pmatrix}: a\in\mathbb Z_p^\times,\quad A\in J_2,\quad v\in\mathbb Q_p^{1\times2} \right\} \simeq\mathbb Q_p^2\rtimes(\mathbb Z_p^\times\times J_2).$$ The classifying maps of $\mathcal V_3$ and $\mathcal V_2$ are the composites of $d$ with the relative maps induced by the inclusion $\rho_3:D% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21AA}% \hookrightarrow\EndAccSupp{}J_3$ and projection $\rho_2:D\to J_2$. The $P_n$-classifying map of $m_n$ is the composite $\mathcal T\xrightarrow{\pi}Y_C\to B_CP_n$ induced by $q_n:G\to P_n$.
  - Explanation: The universal quotient together with the universal surjection gives an exact sequence of slope-zero bundles and local systems of ranks one, three and two, classified through the parabolic subgroup.
- **claim-projective-bundle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-projective-bundle)): Let $X$ be a small v-stack over $\mathop{\mathrm{Spd}}C$, and let $\mathcal W$ be a rank-$(r+1)$ vector bundle on its untilt, with linear v-descent. For $g:\mathbb P(\mathcal W)\to X$, the compatible classes $h_m=c_1(\mathcal O(1))\in H_v^2(\mathbb P(\mathcal W),(\mathbb Z/p^m)(1))$ give equivalences $$\label{eq:projective-finite} \lambda_m: \bigoplus_{j=0}^r(\mathbb Z/p^m)(-j)[-2j] \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } Rg_*(\mathbb Z/p^m).$$ Consequently $$\label{eq:projective-integral} \bigoplus_{j=0}^r R\!\varprojlim_m R\Gamma_v(X,(\mathbb Z/p^m)(-j))[-2j] \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } R\!\varprojlim_m R\Gamma_v(\mathbb P(\mathcal W),\mathbb Z/p^m).$$ The term $j=0$ is the original pullback. Thus pullback is split injective on $H^*_{\mathrm{int}}$ and on $H^*_{\mathrm b}$. These statements are equivariant for actions on the vector bundle, including arithmetic Galois actions.
  - Explanation: Chern classes of the tautological line bundle split the integral cohomology of a projective bundle into twisted copies of the base cohomology, so pullback is split injective, compatibly with Galois.
- **claim-primitive-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-primitive-comparison)): For $n=2,3$, the pullbacks of $e_{P_n}$ and $e_{J_n}$ to $H^3_{\mathrm b}(\mathcal M_n)$ are nonzero. There is a scalar $\kappa_n\in\mathbb Q_p^\times$ such that $$\label{eq:primitive-comparison} e_{P_n}\|_{\mathcal M_n} =\kappa_n\,e_{J_n}\|_{\mathcal M_n}.$$
  - Explanation: Galois weights show that the invariant part of degree-three cohomology of the modification stack is a single line. Both primitive classes pull back to nonzero elements of it, so they are proportional.
- **claim-geometric-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-geometric-transport)): There is a scalar $a\in\mathbb Q_p^\times$ such that the classes from the two stabilizer factors satisfy $$\label{eq:geometric-transport} e_{P_3}\|_{Y_C}=a\,e_{P_2}\|_{Y_C} \quad\text{in }H^3_{\mathrm b}(Y_C).$$
  - Explanation: Pulling back along the rank-one filtration and using injectivity of the projective-bundle pullback, the height-three class equals a scalar multiple of the height-two class on the geometric test space.
- **claim-witt-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-witt-comparison)): The natural constant maps give the commutative square of integral cohomology groups $$\label{eq:witt-square} \begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large] H^i(H,W(k)) \arrow[r,"\sim"] \arrow[d] & H_W^i(Y) \arrow[d] \\ H^i(H,W(C^\flat)) \arrow[r,"\sim"] & H_W^i(Y_C). \end{tikzcd}$$ The actions of $H$ on the two left-hand coefficient rings are trivial. The right vertical map is injective after inverting $p$, for every $i$. Moreover, quotient descent gives a natural identification $$\label{eq:witt-group-identification} R\Gamma_v(Y,W(\mathcal O^\flat)) \simeq R\Gamma(G,W(\widehat L)).$$
  - Explanation: Witt-coefficient cohomology of the quotient stack agrees with group cohomology, and the base-change map to the geometric stack is injective after inverting p, by a bounded finite free complex and flatness.
- **claim-coefficient-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-coefficient-transport)): For the nonzero scalar $a\in\mathbb Q_p^\times$ of <a href="#thm:geometric-transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:geometric-transport">18</a>, one has $$\label{eq:witt-relation} c_3^W=a\,c_2^W \quad\hbox{in }H^3(G,W(\widehat L))[1/p],$$ and hence $$\label{eq:deformation-relation} c_3=a\,c_2 \quad\hbox{in }H^3(G,\pi_0\widehat B^{\square})[1/p].$$ Under <a href="#eq:coefficient-descent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:coefficient-descent">[eq:coefficient-descent]</a>, the preimage of $c_2$ in $$H^3(H,\pi_0E_2^{\square})[1/p]$$ is obtained by inflating $e_{P_2}$ and then including constants.
  - Explanation: Injectivity of Witt base change reflects the geometric proportionality of the two classes back to the stack with Witt coefficients and then to deformation-ring coefficients.
- **claim-constant-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-constant-nonvanishing)): The class $c_2$ is nonzero. Consequently $c_3$ is nonzero as well.
  - Explanation: A continuous additive retraction onto Witt constants shows that the height-two class is nonzero in deformation coefficients; the proportionality then makes the height-three class nonzero too.
- **claim-local-detector** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-detector)): There is a natural map of ordinary spectra $$\label{eq:ordinary-detector} g:L_{K(3)}S\longrightarrow\mathcal D.$$ The spectrum $\mathcal D$ is $K(2)$-local, so $g$ factors through the canonical localization unit: $$\label{eq:detector-factorization} L_{K(3)}S\xrightarrow{\eta}L_{K(2)}L_{K(3)}S \xrightarrow{\overline g}\mathcal D.$$
  - Explanation: The completed theory yields an ordinary spectrum of continuous fixed points which is local at height two, so the detector map factors through the canonical localisation unit.
- **claim-descent-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-descent-convergence)): For the continuous actions used in <a href="#eq:detector-construction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:detector-construction">[eq:detector-construction]</a>, there are natural, strongly convergent spectral sequences $$\label{eq:integral-descent-ss} E_2^{s,t}=H^s(A,\pi_tX) \ \Longrightarrow\ \pi_{t-s}\bigl((X^{hA})(*)\bigr), \qquad 0\leq s\leq d.$$ They give, after exact rationalization, natural strongly convergent spectral sequences with $E_2$-terms $H^s(A,\pi_tX)[1/p]$ and abutments $\pi_{t-s}((X^{hA})(*))[1/p]$. This statement applies to the unbounded even-periodic theories here.
  - Explanation: Finite cohomological dimension gives finite Postnikov windows, hence strongly convergent descent spectral sequences that survive exact rationalisation, even for unbounded even-periodic theories.
- **claim-central-weights** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-central-weights)): For $t\ne0$ and every $s$, one has $$H^s(G_3,\pi_tE_3^{\square})[1/p]=0, \qquad H^s(G,\pi_t\widehat B^{\square})[1/p]=0.$$
  - Explanation: Central scalar automorphisms act by nontrivial powers on the coefficient lines of nonzero degree, so rationalised cohomology vanishes outside coefficient degree zero.
- **claim-nonzero-detector** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-nonzero-detector)): The ordinary map $g$ of <a href="#eq:ordinary-detector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ordinary-detector">[eq:ordinary-detector]</a> is nonzero on rational homotopy in degree $-3$: $$\pi_{-3}L_0g:\pi_{-3}L_0L_{K(3)}S \longrightarrow\pi_{-3}L_0\mathcal D \quad\text{is nonzero}.$$
  - Explanation: The surviving class in group-cohomological degree three and coefficient degree zero gives a nonzero map on rational homotopy in degree minus three.
- **claim-wedge-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-wedge-obstruction)): Suppose $Z\simeq A\vee B$, $L_{K(2)}B=0$, and $\pi_dL_0A=0$. Then $\pi_d(\tau_Z)=0$.
  - Explanation: If a spectrum splits as a wedge with one summand invisible to height-two localisation and the other rationally trivial in the degree, the canonical localisation map vanishes in that degree.
- **claim-continuous-section** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-continuous-as-section)): The additive map $\phi_q-1:R\to R$ has a continuous section as a map of sets. Its condensification is surjective, and its kernel, with the induced topology, is the valued-field completion $E=\bigl(\bigcup_{j\ge0}k((t^{1/p^j}))\bigr)^{\wedge}$. Consequently the canonical map $E\to R^{h\phi_q}$ is an equivalence of derived solid $\mathbb F_p$-modules.
  - Explanation: Frobenius minus identity on the Fréchet ring of convergent series has a continuous set-theoretic section, so it is surjective on solid modules, and its kernel is the completed perfection; solid fixed points are therefore that field.

In particular, invariants of a module in the heart have cohomology only in degrees $0,\\ldots,\\dim G$.*\n\n*Suppose $K$ is bounded below and an operator $\\gamma$ commutes with $G$. If $\\gamma$ acts by $\\lambda_j\\in\\mathbb Q_p$ on $H^j(K)(*)[1/p]$, then it acts by the same scalar on every term in the $j$th row of the rationalized descent spectral sequence $$H^s\\bigl(G,H^j(K)\\bigr)(*)[1/p]\n       \\ \\Longrightarrow\\ H^{s+j}\\bigl(R\\Gamma(G,K)(*)\\bigr)[1/p].$$ For $K=\\mathscr C_X$, the abutment is $H^{s+j}_{\\mathrm b}([X/G])$.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G$ be a compact $p$-adic analytic group without elements of order $p$, and let $\\Lambda_G=\\mathbb Z_p[[G]]$. There is a finite resolution $P_\\bullet\\to\\mathbb Z_p$ by finitely generated projective profinite $\\Lambda_G$-modules. Its length can be taken to be $\\dim G$. For any derived-solid $\\mathbb Z_p$-complex $K$ with a continuous $G$-action, the point-valued derived invariants are computed by $$\\label{eq:finite-resolution}\n R\\Gamma(G,K)(*)\\simeq\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\Lambda_G}(P_\\bullet,K).$$ Here the right side means the finite total complex of morphisms in solid modules. In particular, invariants of a module in the heart have cohomology only in degrees $0,\\ldots,\\dim G$.*\n\n*Suppose $K$ is bounded below and an operator $\\gamma$ commutes with $G$. If $\\gamma$ acts by $\\lambda_j\\in\\mathbb Q_p$ on $H^j(K)(*)[1/p]$, then it acts by the same scalar on every term in the $j$th row of the rationalized descent spectral sequence $$H^s\\bigl(G,H^j(K)\\bigr)(*)[1/p]\n       \\ \\Longrightarrow\\ H^{s+j}\\bigl(R\\Gamma(G,K)(*)\\bigr)[1/p].$$ For $K=\\mathscr C_X$, the abutment is $H^{s+j}_{\\mathrm b}([X/G])$.",
      "explanation": "A compact p-adic analytic group without p-torsion has a finite projective resolution of the trivial module over its completed group ring. This computes derived invariants of solid coefficients by a finite complex and shows that commuting scalar operators act row by row in the descent spectral sequence.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-group-classes",
        "exact_quote": "For $G=P_n$ or $J_n$, where $n=2,3$, $$H^1_{\\mathrm b}(G)=\\mathbb Q_p,\n \\qquad H^2_{\\mathrm b}(G)=0,\n \\qquad H^3_{\\mathrm b}(G)=\\mathbb Q_p.$$ For $n=2,3$ and every compact open subgroup $K\\subset J_n$, restriction gives an isomorphism in every degree, with the compact comparison identifying its target: $$H^*_{\\mathrm b}(J_n)\\xrightarrow{\\mathrm{res}}H^*_{\\mathrm b}(K)\n \\cong H^*(\\mathfrak{gl}_n(\\mathbb Q_p),\\mathbb Q_p).$$ In degree three the block inclusion $J_2%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21AA}%\n  \\hookrightarrow\\EndAccSupp{}J_3$ induces a nonzero map.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-drinfeld",
        "exact_quote": "For $0\\leq j\\leq d$ there are compatible $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1}(\\mathbb Q_p)\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gal}}(\\overline\\mathbb Q_p/\\mathbb Q_p)$-equivariant isomorphisms of groups $$H^j_{\\mathrm{int}}(\\Omega_C^d)(j)\n       \\simeq\\operatorname{Sp}_j(\\mathbb Z_p)^*,\n \\qquad\n H^j_{\\mathrm{\\acute et}}(\\Omega_C^d,\\mathbb F_p(j))\n       \\simeq\\operatorname{Sp}_j(\\mathbb F_p)^*.$$ Here $\\operatorname{Sp}_j$ is the generalized Steinberg representation of (Colmez, Dospinescu, and Nizioł 2021, sec. 4.1) [[CDN2021](#ref-CDN2021)], the star denotes its continuous coefficient-linear dual with weak topology, and the right sides have trivial Galois action. These groups vanish for $j>d$, and $H^0_{\\mathrm{int}}(\\Omega_C^d)=\\mathbb Z_p$ by constants. In particular, on the untwisted integral group in degree $j$, an arithmetic Galois element $\\gamma$ acts by $\\chi_{\\mathrm{cyc}}(\\gamma)^{-j}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-drinfeld",
          "exact_quote": "For $0\\leq j\\leq d$ there are compatible $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1}(\\mathbb Q_p)\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gal}}(\\overline\\mathbb Q_p/\\mathbb Q_p)$-equivariant isomorphisms of groups $$H^j_{\\mathrm{int}}(\\Omega_C^d)(j)\n       \\simeq\\operatorname{Sp}_j(\\mathbb Z_p)^*,\n \\qquad\n H^j_{\\mathrm{\\acute et}}(\\Omega_C^d,\\mathbb F_p(j))\n       \\simeq\\operatorname{Sp}_j(\\mathbb F_p)^*.$$ Here $\\operatorname{Sp}_j$ is the generalized Steinberg representation of (Colmez, Dospinescu, and Nizioł 2021, sec. 4.1) [[CDN2021](#ref-CDN2021)], the star denotes its continuous coefficient-linear dual with weak topology, and the right sides have trivial Galois action. These groups vanish for $j>d$, and $H^0_{\\mathrm{int}}(\\Omega_C^d)=\\mathbb Z_p$ by constants. In particular, on the untwisted integral group in degree $j$, an arithmetic Galois element $\\gamma$ acts by $\\chi_{\\mathrm{cyc}}(\\gamma)^{-j}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $0\\leq j\\leq d$ there are compatible $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1}(\\mathbb Q_p)\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gal}}(\\overline\\mathbb Q_p/\\mathbb Q_p)$-equivariant isomorphisms of groups $$H^j_{\\mathrm{int}}(\\Omega_C^d)(j)\n       \\simeq\\operatorname{Sp}_j(\\mathbb Z_p)^*,\n \\qquad\n H^j_{\\mathrm{\\acute et}}(\\Omega_C^d,\\mathbb F_p(j))\n       \\simeq\\operatorname{Sp}_j(\\mathbb F_p)^*.$$ Here $\\operatorname{Sp}_j$ is the generalized Steinberg representation of (Colmez, Dospinescu, and Nizioł 2021, sec. 4.1) [[CDN2021](#ref-CDN2021)], the star denotes its continuous coefficient-linear dual with weak topology, and the right sides have trivial Galois action.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-tower",
        "exact_quote": "The field $\\widehat L$ is perfectoid of characteristic $p$, and is isomorphic, as a valued field over $k$, to the completed perfection of a Laurent-series field: $$\\widehat L\\simeq\n \\left(\\bigcup_{r\\ge0}k((z^{1/p^r}))\\right)^{\\wedge}.$$ There is a $G$-equivariant identification of v-sheaves over $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spd}}k$ $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spd}}\\widehat L\\simeq\n \\operatorname{Surj}(\\mathcal E_3^0,\\mathcal E_2^0).$$ Consequently $$Y=[\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spd}}\\widehat L/G]$$ classifies surjections $\\mathcal E_3\\twoheadrightarrow\\mathcal E_2$ between $P_n$-structured forms. In particular $Y$ carries the universal surjection used below.*\n\n*Let $S_3=\\ker N_3$ and $T_3=P_3/S_3=\\mathbb Z_p^{\\times}$. Then $$[\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spd}}\\widehat L/S_3]\n \\simeq\\operatorname{BC}(-1/2)^*$$ as v-sheaves, with the following residual $P_2\\times T_3$-action. On extension classes in $\\operatorname{Ext}^1(\\mathcal E_2^0,\\mathcal O)$, write $\\lambda_*$ for pushout by $\\lambda$ on $\\mathcal O$ and $b^*$ for pullback by $b$ on $\\mathcal E_2^0$. For the framing action $f%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}%\n  \\mapsto\\EndAccSupp{}bfg^{-1}$ and $a=N_3(g)$, the action is $$(b,a)\\cdot\\xi\n   =\\bigl(aN_2(b)^{-1}\\bigr)_*(b^{-1})^*\\xi.$$ In particular, the $T_3$-action is pushout by $a$ on the kernel. Only this restriction of the action on the extension chart is used below.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-completed-theory",
        "exact_quote": "There are continuous $G$-equivariant maps of solid $E_\\infty$-rings $$\\label{eq:completed-map}\n E_3^{\\square}\\longrightarrow\\widehat B^{\\square}\n       \\longleftarrow E_2^{\\square},$$ where $G$ acts through $P_3$ on the first source and through $P_2$ on the second. The right arrow is canonical connected base change. The target is even periodic, with $$\\pi_0\\widehat B^{\\square}(*)\\simeq W(\\widehat L)[[u'_1]].$$ Invariantly, its coefficients are complete base changes of the height-two deformation ring and its invertible even coefficient lines. The natural map $W(\\widehat L)\\to\\pi_0\\widehat B^{\\square}$ is $G$-equivariant. Every profinite evaluation $\\widehat B^{\\square}(T)$ is an ordinary $K(2)$-local spectrum.*\n\n\n*The second map in <a href=\"#eq:completed-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:completed-map\">[eq:completed-map]</a> induces an $H$-equivariant equivalence $$E_2^{\\square}\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n       (\\widehat B^{\\square})^{hP_3^1},$$ where $\\overline Z$ acts trivially on the source. Moreover, for every $t\\in\\mathbb Z$ it induces an equivalence on the individual coefficient complexes $$\\pi_tE_2^{\\square}\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n       R\\Gamma(P_3^1,\\pi_t\\widehat B^{\\square}).$$ In particular, for $s\\ge0$ and all $t$ there are natural isomorphisms $$\\label{eq:coefficient-descent}\n H^s(H,\\pi_tE_2^{\\square})\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n H^s(G,\\pi_t\\widehat B^{\\square}).$$ These comparisons preserve the coefficient maps from Witt constants.*\n\n*Finally, put $\\Delta=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gal}}(k/\\mathbb F_p)$ and $G_n=P_n\\rtimes\\Delta$. The ordinary stabilizer-descent identification is $$\\bigl((E_n^{\\square})^{hG_n}\\bigr)(*)\\simeq L_{K(n)}S,\n \\qquad n=2,3,$$ compatibly with the canonical maps to $E_n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

The ordinary stabilizer-descent identification is $$\\bigl((E_n^{\\square})^{hG_n}\\bigr)(*)\\simeq L_{K(n)}S,\n \\qquad n=2,3,$$ compatibly with the canonical maps to $E_n$.",

      "exact_statement": "There are continuous $G$-equivariant maps of solid $E_\\infty$-rings $$\\label{eq:completed-map}\n E_3^{\\square}\\longrightarrow\\widehat B^{\\square}\n       \\longleftarrow E_2^{\\square},$$ where $G$ acts through $P_3$ on the first source and through $P_2$ on the second. The right arrow is canonical connected base change. The target is even periodic, with $$\\pi_0\\widehat B^{\\square}(*)\\simeq W(\\widehat L)[[u'_1]].$$ Invariantly, its coefficients are complete base changes of the height-two deformation ring and its invertible even coefficient lines. The natural map $W(\\widehat L)\\to\\pi_0\\widehat B^{\\square}$ is $G$-equivariant. Every profinite evaluation $\\widehat B^{\\square}(T)$ is an ordinary $K(2)$-local spectrum.*\n\n\n*The second map in <a href=\"#eq:completed-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:completed-map\">[eq:completed-map]</a> induces an $H$-equivariant equivalence $$E_2^{\\square}\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n       (\\widehat B^{\\square})^{hP_3^1},$$ where $\\overline Z$ acts trivially on the source. Moreover, for every $t\\in\\mathbb Z$ it induces an equivalence on the individual coefficient complexes $$\\pi_tE_2^{\\square}\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n       R\\Gamma(P_3^1,\\pi_t\\widehat B^{\\square}).$$ In particular, for $s\\ge0$ and all $t$ there are natural isomorphisms $$\\label{eq:coefficient-descent}\n H^s(H,\\pi_tE_2^{\\square})\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n H^s(G,\\pi_t\\widehat B^{\\square}).$$ These comparisons preserve the coefficient maps from Witt constants.*\n\n*Finally, put $\\Delta=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gal}}(k/\\mathbb F_p)$ and $G_n=P_n\\rtimes\\Delta$. The ordinary stabilizer-descent identification is $$\\bigl((E_n^{\\square})^{hG_n}\\bigr)(*)\\simeq L_{K(n)}S,\n \\qquad n=2,3,$$ compatibly with the canonical maps to $E_n$.",
      "explanation": "There is a completed even-periodic height-two Lubin–Tate theory receiving equivariant ring maps from both Morava E-theories. Taking fixed points of the first subgroup recovers the height-two theory coefficientwise, and the ordinary stabiliser descent recovers the local sphere.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-completed-oriented-map",
        "exact_quote": "Put $F=\\widehat L(*)$, regarded here as the ordinary completed field. For a nonempty profinite set $T$, put $R_T=C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,F)$ and let $\\Gamma_T=\\Gamma^{\\mathrm{un}}_{3,R_T}$ be the full completed $p$-divisible group. Let $C_T$ be the base change of the connected term in the uniquely split sequence over $F$. It is identified with $(\\Gamma_2)_{R_T}$ by the fixed tautological Gross–Torii formal isomorphism. There is a continuous adic $E_\\infty$-map $$r_T:E(R_T,\\Gamma_T)\\longrightarrow E(R_T,C_T).$$ The maps are natural for profinite pullbacks and every continuous $G=P_3\\times P_2$ family of pair maps. With the terminal value at the empty parameter set, pointwise application and hypersheafification give a continuous $G$-equivariant map of condensed $E_\\infty$-rings $$r^{\\mathrm{cond}}:\n E^{\\mathrm{cond}}(\\widehat L,\\Gamma^{\\mathrm{un}}_{3,\\widehat L})\n \\longrightarrow E^{\\mathrm{cond}}(\\widehat L,C_{\\widehat L}).$$*\n\n*Let $b_T:E_2\\to E(R_T,\\Gamma_T)$ be the pointwise ordinary $E_2$ tensor-factor map followed by completion, and let $c_T:E_2\\to E(R_T,C_T)$ be the canonical connected map induced by $k\\to R_T$. Then $r_Tb_T\\simeq c_T$, naturally for the same parameters and pair families. This homotopy preserves the connected residue tag and orientation, and hence its coefficient and even-line maps. After condensation and solid localization, it gives $$r^{\\mathrm{cond}}b^\\square\\simeq c^\\square$$ as continuous $G$-equivariant maps of underlying solid spectra, where $b^\\square$ is the tensor-factor map followed by completion and $c^\\square$ is the canonical connected base-change map.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-drinfeld-locus",
        "exact_quote": "The upper modification $\\mathcal E_\\ell$ is semistable of slope $1/n$ if and only if $\\ell$ lies in no proper $\\mathbb Q_p$-rational subspace. Consequently its semistable locus is $$\\Omega_C^{n-1}\n =\\mathbb P_C^{n-1}\\setminus\n       \\bigcup_{\\substack{W\\subset\\mathbb Q_p^n\\\\ \\dim W=n-1}}\\mathbb P(W_C).$$ On this locus $\\mathcal E_\\ell$ is a form of $\\mathcal E_n^0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-drinfeld-locus",
          "exact_quote": "The upper modification $\\mathcal E_\\ell$ is semistable of slope $1/n$ if and only if $\\ell$ lies in no proper $\\mathbb Q_p$-rational subspace.           "source_id": "PM-318.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The upper modification $\\mathcal E_\\ell$ is semistable of slope $1/n$ if and only if $\\ell$ lies in no proper $\\mathbb Q_p$-rational subspace.       "explanation": "An upper modification of the trivial bundle is semistable exactly when its direction line avoids every proper rational subspace, so the semistable locus is Drinfeld space.",

There are $G_F$-equivariant equivalences of v-stacks $$\\label{eq:two-realizations}\n \\mathcal M_n\n \\simeq [\\mathbb P(i_\\infty^*\\mathcal E_n^0)/P_n]\n \\simeq [\\mathcal Z_n/(P_n\\times J_n)]\n \\simeq [\\Omega_C^{n-1}/J_n].$$ Its framing torsor defines $\\mathcal M_n\\to B_CP_n$, and its universal lower modification defines the indicated map $\\mathcal M_n\\to B_CJ_n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-filtered-modification",
        "exact_quote": "The tautological quotient of $\\mathcal E_2$ and its composite with $f$ give maps $m_n:\\mathcal T\\to\\mathcal M_n$ and an exact sequence $$\\label{eq:filtered-modification}\n 0\\longrightarrow\\ker(f)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal V_3\n       \\longrightarrow\\mathcal V_2\\longrightarrow0 .$$ These are slope-zero bundles. Their corresponding rational local systems form an exact sequence of ranks $1,3,2$. With the induced determinant lattices this sequence is classified by a map $d:\\mathcal T\\to B_CD$, where $$D=\\left\\{\n \\begin{pmatrix}a&v\\\\0&A\\end{pmatrix}:\n       a\\in\\mathbb Z_p^\\times,\\quad A\\in J_2,\\quad v\\in\\mathbb Q_p^{1\\times2}\n       \\right\\}\n   \\simeq\\mathbb Q_p^2\\rtimes(\\mathbb Z_p^\\times\\times J_2).$$ The classifying maps of $\\mathcal V_3$ and $\\mathcal V_2$ are the composites of $d$ with the relative maps induced by the inclusion $\\rho_3:D%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21AA}%\n  \\hookrightarrow\\EndAccSupp{}J_3$ and projection $\\rho_2:D\\to J_2$. The $P_n$-classifying map of $m_n$ is the composite $\\mathcal T\\xrightarrow{\\pi}Y_C\\to B_CP_n$ induced by $q_n:G\\to P_n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-filtered-modification",
          "exact_quote": "The tautological quotient of $\\mathcal E_2$ and its composite with $f$ give maps $m_n:\\mathcal T\\to\\mathcal M_n$ and an exact sequence $$\\label{eq:filtered-modification}\n 0\\longrightarrow\\ker(f)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal V_3\n       \\longrightarrow\\mathcal V_2\\longrightarrow0 .$$ These are slope-zero bundles. Their corresponding rational local systems form an exact sequence of ranks $1,3,2$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-projective-bundle",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a small v-stack over $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spd}}C$, and let $\\mathcal W$ be a rank-$(r+1)$ vector bundle on its untilt, with linear v-descent. For $g:\\mathbb P(\\mathcal W)\\to X$, the compatible classes $h_m=c_1(\\mathcal O(1))\\in H_v^2(\\mathbb P(\\mathcal W),(\\mathbb Z/p^m)(1))$ give equivalences $$\\label{eq:projective-finite}\n \\lambda_m:\n \\bigoplus_{j=0}^r(\\mathbb Z/p^m)(-j)[-2j]\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ } Rg_*(\\mathbb Z/p^m).$$ Consequently $$\\label{eq:projective-integral}\n \\bigoplus_{j=0}^r\n  R\\!\\varprojlim_m R\\Gamma_v(X,(\\mathbb Z/p^m)(-j))[-2j]\n \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n  R\\!\\varprojlim_m R\\Gamma_v(\\mathbb P(\\mathcal W),\\mathbb Z/p^m).$$ The term $j=0$ is the original pullback. Thus pullback is split injective on $H^*_{\\mathrm{int}}$ and on $H^*_{\\mathrm b}$. These statements are equivariant for actions on the vector bundle, including arithmetic Galois actions.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-projective-bundle",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a small v-stack over $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spd}}C$, and let $\\mathcal W$ be a rank-$(r+1)$ vector bundle on its untilt, with linear v-descent.       "exact_statement": "Let $X$ be a small v-stack over $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spd}}C$, and let $\\mathcal W$ be a rank-$(r+1)$ vector bundle on its untilt, with linear v-descent. For $g:\\mathbb P(\\mathcal W)\\to X$, the compatible classes $h_m=c_1(\\mathcal O(1))\\in H_v^2(\\mathbb P(\\mathcal W),(\\mathbb Z/p^m)(1))$ give equivalences $$\\label{eq:projective-finite}\n \\lambda_m:\n \\bigoplus_{j=0}^r(\\mathbb Z/p^m)(-j)[-2j]\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ } Rg_*(\\mathbb Z/p^m).$$ Consequently $$\\label{eq:projective-integral}\n \\bigoplus_{j=0}^r\n  R\\!\\varprojlim_m R\\Gamma_v(X,(\\mathbb Z/p^m)(-j))[-2j]\n \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n  R\\!\\varprojlim_m R\\Gamma_v(\\mathbb P(\\mathcal W),\\mathbb Z/p^m).$$ The term $j=0$ is the original pullback. Thus pullback is split injective on $H^*_{\\mathrm{int}}$ and on $H^*_{\\mathrm b}$. These statements are equivariant for actions on the vector bundle, including arithmetic Galois actions.",

Its condensification is surjective, and its kernel, with the induced topology, is the valued-field completion $E=\\bigl(\\bigcup_{j\\ge0}k((t^{1/p^j}))\\bigr)^{\\wedge}$. Consequently the canonical map $E\\to R^{h\\phi_q}$ is an equivalence of derived solid $\\mathbb F_p$-modules.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The additive map $\\phi_q-1:R\\to R$ has a continuous section as a map of sets. Its condensification is surjective, and its kernel, with the induced topology, is the valued-field completion $E=\\bigl(\\bigcup_{j\\ge0}k((t^{1/p^j}))\\bigr)^{\\wedge}$. Consequently the canonical map $E\\to R^{h\\phi_q}$ is an equivalence of derived solid $\\mathbb F_p$-modules.",
      "explanation": "Frobenius minus identity on the Fréchet ring of convergent series has a continuous set-theoretic section, so it is surjective on solid modules, and its kernel is the completed perfection; solid fixed points are therefore that field.",

        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **integral coefficient level** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Put $\Lambda_m=\mathbb Z/p^m$.
- **determinant lattice** ([source](#sec:modifications)): A *determinant lattice* of a slope-zero rank-$n$ bundle means a $\mathbb Z_p$-lattice in its rank-one determinant local system.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-group-classes** [claim](#stmt-prop-group-classes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-group-classes): Compare compact open subgroups with Lie algebra cohomology, use a finite Chevalley–Eilenberg calculation, then pass to the linear group through the cellular resolution of the affine building. -> Low-degree classes of the two stabiliser families and nonzero block restriction.
- **step-solid-resolutions** [claim](#stmt-lem-solid-resolutions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-solid-resolutions): Build a finite projective resolution over the completed group ring and show it stays projective in solid modules. -> Finite complexes computing derived invariants.
- **step-parabolic** [claim](#stmt-lem-parabolic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-parabolic): Show pro-zero behaviour of unipotent cohomology and descend through the split extension. -> Proportionality of restricted rank-three and rank-two classes.
- **step-tower** [claim](#stmt-prop-tower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tower): Import the characteristic-p tower description and compute the residual action by functoriality of extensions. -> Moduli description of the completed field.
- **step-constants** [claim](#stmt-prop-constant-acyclicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-constant-acyclicity): Use a fibre sequence with a nontrivial character line and average over the finite cyclic quotient. -> Constants are acyclic after norm-unit invariants.
- **step-frobenius-section** [claim](#stmt-lem-continuous-as-section) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-continuous-as-section): Construct an explicit continuous section of Frobenius minus identity. -> Surjectivity on solid modules.
- **step-completed-theory** [claim](#stmt-prop-completed-theory) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-completed-oriented-map-2): Combine connected endpoint descent, the oriented section from the appendix and point-evaluation of solid localisation. -> Equivariant maps from both Morava theories into the completed theory.
- **step-oriented-map** [claim](#stmt-lem-completed-oriented-map) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-completed-oriented-map): Split the connected-étale sequence over the perfect field and use corepresentability of oriented deformations. -> Retraction onto the connected theory.
- **step-modifications** [claim](#stmt-prop-two-realizations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-two-realizations): Compare lower and upper modifications and the common torsor of frames. -> Two presentations of the modification stack.
- **step-lower-upper** [claim](#stmt-lem-drinfeld-locus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-drinfeld-locus): Slope computations for destabilising subbundles of the modified bundles. -> Semistability criteria.
- **step-projective-bundle** [claim](#stmt-lem-projective-bundle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-projective-bundle): Chern-class splitting via Riemann–Hilbert comparison, descent and induction on the coefficient level. -> Split injectivity of pullback.
- **step-primitive-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-primitive-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-primitive-comparison): Galois weights on the projective side and a descent spectral sequence on the Drinfeld side. -> Proportional nonzero primitive classes.
- **step-geometric-transport** [claim](#stmt-thm-geometric-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-geometric-transport): Chain of equalities along the filtration, then injectivity of pullback. -> Relation between the two classes on the geometric test space.
- **step-witt** [claim](#stmt-prop-witt-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-witt-comparison): Devissage over Witt truncations and a bounded finite free complex with flat base change. -> Injective base change after inverting p.
- **step-coefficient-transport** [claim](#stmt-prop-coefficient-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-coefficient-transport): Reflect the geometric relation through Witt injectivity. -> Relation in deformation coefficients.
- **step-constant-nonvanishing** [claim](#stmt-lem-constant-nonvanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-constant-nonvanishing): Additive retraction onto Witt constants. -> Nonzero class in deformation coefficients.
- **step-detection** [claim](#stmt-prop-nonzero-detector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nonzero-detector): Descent spectral sequences with only one surviving coefficient row. -> Nonzero map on rational homotopy in degree minus three.
- **step-convergence** [claim](#stmt-lem-descent-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-descent-convergence): Finite Postnikov windows and exact couple. -> Strongly convergent rational spectral sequences.
- **step-central-weights** [claim](#stmt-lem-central-weights) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-central-weights): A central scalar acts trivially on cohomology but by a nontrivial scalar on coefficients. -> Vanishing outside coefficient degree zero.
- **step-local-detector** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-detector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-detector): Compose restriction, inflation and the completed map; use locality of limits. -> Detector factoring through the localisation unit.
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nonzero-detector-2): Rationalise the factorisation and contradict the nonzero detector. -> Main nonvanishing theorem.
- **step-wedge** [claim](#stmt-lem-wedge-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-wedge-obstruction): Naturality of the localisation map on wedge summands. -> Vanishing for wedge-shaped spectra.
- **step-corollary** [claim](#stmt-cor-strong) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-wedge-obstruction-2): Apply the wedge lemma to the proposed wedge and compare with the main theorem by naturality. -> No equivalence with the proposed wedge.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-nonvanishing**: For primes at least five, the canonical rationalized comparison map from the height-three Morava-local sphere to its height-two localization is nonzero on rational homotopy in degree minus three.
- **inv-no-underlying-equivalence**: No equivalence of E(2)-local spectra exists between the localized height-three overlap and the proposed wedge, whether or not summand maps are prescribed.
- **inv-wedge-vanishing**: If a spectrum splits as a wedge with a K(2)-acyclic summand and a summand with vanishing rational homotopy in degree d, then the canonical comparison map vanishes on homotopy in degree d.
- **inv-detector-nonzero**: The ordinary detecting map into the K(2)-local fixed-point spectrum is nonzero on rational homotopy in degree minus three.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-small-primes**: Apply the nonvanishing result or its wedge consequence at the primes two or three. -> The proof relies on absence of p-torsion in the stabilizer groups and on a nontrivial character, which are not available at small primes; the statement is not claimed there.
- **anti-weak-splitting**: Conclude from the wedge obstruction that weak chromatic splitting fails at height three. -> The result rules out a wedge equivalence only; a retraction of the canonical unit is not decided.
- **anti-filtration**: Infer that no finite filtration with the classical cofibers can assemble the overlap. -> A filtration may carry nonzero attaching maps that a wedge discards, so the obstruction to a wedge does not transfer.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are existence and non-existence statements about rational homotopy of chromatic localizations, proved by descent spectral sequences, solid cohomology and geometric transport; the paper gives no finite procedure or explicit construction whose output could be executed and checked. The finite Chevalley-Eilenberg minors in the appendix are only auxiliary certificates and do not compute the stated theorems.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves a structural result in stable homotopy theory, a rational obstruction to the proposed wedge decomposition. It specifies no program, interface or executable procedure, so no software component can be derived from it. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No dynamical system, state space or discrete model that could be simulated appears. The objects are localizations of spectra, and the argument only compares rational homotopy groups. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only finite computations are the explicit minors certifying low-rank Lie algebra cohomology. They are verification aids inside the proof, not a reusable tool, and they do not support a tooling contract. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main theorem is non-constructive. Nonvanishing is established through descent spectral sequences and a continuous retraction, and no finite procedure with inputs and outputs is stated. The resolution lemma gives only an existence statement with a length bound. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The affine building appears as a contractible simplicial complex with a chamber as fundamental domain, serving a cellular resolution argument. It is not proposed as a data structure with operations or costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No arithmetic kernel, dataflow or circuit-level quantity is studied. The result concerns the vanishing of a canonical map between localized spectra at a prime at least five. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Perfectoid fields, Witt vectors and p-adic groups occur only as coefficient and descent tools. No hardness assumption, protocol or security reduction is formulated. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data, model, loss or learning procedure is present. The detection argument is a factorization through a locality property of a homotopy-fixed-point spectrum. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no objective function, constraint set or optimization problem. The proof builds finite free presentations purely to compute derived invariants of groups. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Descent here is the mathematical gluing of cohomology along covers and torsors. It has no relation to replication, consensus or message passing. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only transferable value is as a negative guard for formal reasoning about chromatic splitting. A wedge decomposition cannot be assumed at height three, while a weak splitting with a unit retraction stays open. This is a scope warning and not a construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-prime-range** (hypothesis): The nonvanishing theorem and the wedge refutation are stated and proved only for primes at least five. The argument uses absence of p-torsion in the stabilizer groups and a nontrivial character on a finite cyclic quotient. Blocked use: ['Applying the nonvanishing result or the wedge refutation at the primes two or three', 'Treating the result as a statement about all primes']
- **limit-strong-vs-weak-splitting** (formal_scope): The corollary rules out an equivalence of E(2)-local spectra with the proposed wedge. It does not decide whether a retraction of the canonical unit exists, which is the weak chromatic splitting question. Blocked use: ['Concluding that weak chromatic splitting fails at height three', 'Reading the result as a statement about the unit summand map only']
- **limit-filtration-not-excluded** (counterexample_scope): The obstruction applies to a split sum. A finite filtration with the same cofibers may keep nonzero attaching maps that a wedge would discard, so it is not excluded. Blocked use: ['Inferring that no finite filtration with the classical cofibers can assemble the overlap', 'Using the corollary to rule out nonsplit assemblies']
- **limit-completed-target** (formal_scope): Detection uses the completed Lubin-Tate theory. The paper does not assert an equivalence from the uncompleted fixed points to that target; the uncompleted half-sphere statement remains conjectural. Blocked use: ['Using the uncompleted half-sphere equivalence as a proved result', 'Replacing the completed detector by uncompleted fixed points']
- **limit-no-p-torsion** (hypothesis): The finite resolution by projective modules over the completed group ring needs a compact p-adic analytic group without elements of order p. All descent and convergence statements rely on this finite cohomological bound. Blocked use: ['Applying the finite-resolution lemma to groups with p-torsion', 'Assuming a uniform cohomological bound without this hypothesis']
- **limit-order-of-inversion** (edge_case): Cohomology is formed integrally, with the derived limit taken first and p inverted afterwards. Inverting p before cohomology can change the result for noncompact spaces or groups. Blocked use: ['Computing the cohomology groups with p inverted in the coefficients first', 'Identifying the cohomology with continuous cohomology for noncompact groups']
- **limit-nonconstructive-scalar** (nonconstructive): The scalar relating the two degree-three classes is shown to exist and be nonzero, but no procedure for computing it is given. Blocked use: ['Treating the scalar as a computable quantity', 'Building an algorithm that outputs the scalar']
- **limit-geometric-transport-only** (formal_scope): The proportionality of classes is an equality on the geometric test space and does not itself give nonvanishing there. Nonvanishing needs the later transfer through Witt coefficients and the retraction onto constants. Blocked use: ['Deducing nonvanishing of the classes from the proportionality alone']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every prime <i>p</i> ≥ 5, the canonical map $`L_0L_{K(3)}S\to L_0L_{K(2)}L_{K(3)}S`$ for the sphere spectrum <i>S</i> is nonzero on <i>π</i><sub>−3</sub>. Consequently, the height-three strong chromatic splitting formula is false in this range, even as an equivalence of underlying $`E(2)`$-local spectra without specified summand maps.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $S$ be the sphere spectrum, fix a prime $p$, and write $S_p^\wedge$ for its $p$-completion. We use $L_i$ for localization at Johnson–Wilson theory $E(i)$ and $L_{K(i)}$ for localization at Morava $K$-theory of height $i$, all at $p$. In particular, $L_0$ is rationalization. Chromatic splitting concerns the lower-height overlap $L_{n-1}L_{K(n)}S$ in the fracture square for $L_nS$. Hopkins’ strong formulation, recorded by Hovey (Hovey 1995, Conjecture 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Hovey1995](#ref-Hovey1995)], prescribes an assembly of this overlap from localized spheres.

<a id="eq:proposed-wedge"></a>
At height three, the proposed underlying spectrum is $$\label{eq:proposed-wedge}
\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal W_3={}&L_2\bigl(S_p^\wedge\vee\Sigma^{-1}S_p^\wedge\bigr)\\
 &{}\vee L_1\bigl(\Sigma^{-3}S_p^\wedge\vee\Sigma^{-4}S_p^\wedge\bigr)\\
 &{}\vee L_0\bigl(\Sigma^{-5}S_p^\wedge\vee\Sigma^{-6}S_p^\wedge
             \vee\Sigma^{-8}S_p^\wedge\vee\Sigma^{-9}S_p^\wedge\bigr).
\end{aligned}$$ The strong prediction is an equivalence $L_2L_{K(3)}S\simeq\mathcal W_3$, with specified maps, including the canonical unit on the first summand. The revised formulation of Barthel–Beaudry retains this odd-prime prediction (Barthel and Beaudry 2019, Conjecture 6.3 and Remark 6.4) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[BB2019](#ref-BB2019)]. We disprove even the underlying equivalence of $E(2)$-local spectra.

The obstruction is a natural map. For every spectrum $X$, the $K(2)$-localization unit induces $$\tau_X:L_0X\longrightarrow L_0L_{K(2)}X.$$

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every prime $p\geq5$, the canonical map <a id="eq:main-map"></a>$$\label{eq:main-map}
 \tau_{L_{K(3)}S}:L_0L_{K(3)}S\longrightarrow
                         L_0L_{K(2)}L_{K(3)}S$$ is nonzero on $\pi_{-3}$.*

</div>

<a id="stmt-cor-strong"></a>
<div id="cor:strong" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *For every prime $p\geq5$, there is no equivalence of $E(2)$-local spectra $L_2L_{K(3)}S\simeq\mathcal W_3$, with $\mathcal W_3$ as in <a href="#eq:proposed-wedge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:proposed-wedge">[eq:proposed-wedge]</a>. Thus the height-three strong chromatic splitting formula is false in this range, whether or not an equivalence is required to preserve its specified unit summand.*

</div>

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Why the canonical map matters

The rational homotopy groups of the $K(n)$-local sphere are known for every positive height $n$ and every prime. Barthel–Schlank–Stapleton–Weinstein identify their algebra with an exterior algebra over $\mathbb Q_p$, with primitive generators in degrees $1-2i$, $1\leq i\leq n$ (Barthel et al. 2025, Theorem A). At height three the resulting degrees are $$0,-1,-3,-4,-5,-6,-8,-9,$$ exactly the shifts appearing in <a href="#eq:proposed-wedge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:proposed-wedge">[eq:proposed-wedge]</a>. Thus the rational degree pattern does not distinguish the proposed wedge from the actual overlap.

The natural transformation $\tau$ does distinguish them. In $\mathcal W_3$, a degree-$-3$ class can occur only in the lower-height part, which is $K(2)$-acyclic. The two top-height summands have no rational homotopy in that degree. It follows that $\tau_{\mathcal W_3}$ vanishes on $\pi_{-3}$. The formal argument in <a href="#sec:wedge-obstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:wedge-obstruction">8</a> also compares the maps for $L_{K(3)}S$ and $L_2L_{K(3)}S$. Naturality makes the conclusion valid for any hypothetical equivalence, with no assumption on its individual summand maps.

At height two, Hovey records Hopkins’ splitting for $p>3$, based on Shimomura–Yabe’s calculations (Hovey 1995, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Hovey1995](#ref-Hovey1995)] <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ShimomuraYabe1995](#ref-ShimomuraYabe1995)], (Shimomura and Yabe 1995, Theorem 2.4). Behrens later reorganized those calculations and displayed the predicted overlap homotopy groups (Behrens 2012, Remark 7.8) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Behrens2012](#ref-Behrens2012)]. Goerss–Henn–Mahowald proved the splitting at $p=3$ (Goerss, Henn, and Mahowald 2014, Theorem 5.11) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[GHM2014](#ref-GHM2014)]. At $p=2$, Beaudry disproved the original formula (Beaudry 2017, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Beaudry2017](#ref-Beaudry2017)], while Beaudry–Goerss–Henn subsequently proved a refined splitting with additional Moore-spectrum summands and retained the weak unit splitting (Beaudry, Goerss, and Henn 2022, Theorem 1.1.6) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BGH2022](#ref-BGH2022)].

The canonical map already controls the height-two argument of Goerss–Henn–Mahowald. At $p=3$, their proof identifies the rational map from the $K(2)$-local sphere to its $K(1)$-localization with projection onto the degrees zero and minus one, followed by canonical localization; the fracture square then reconstructs the splitting (Goerss, Henn, and Mahowald 2014, proof of Theorem 5.11, p. 1288) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[GHM2014](#ref-GHM2014)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> finds a nonzero degree-minus-three component at height three, which obstructs $\mathcal W_3$. A finite filtration with these cofibers may retain nonzero attaching maps that a wedge would discard. The existence of a retraction of the canonical unit in weak chromatic splitting remains a separate question.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### A detecting spectrum

The proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> has two tasks: construct a map from $L_{K(3)}S$ to an ordinary $K(2)$-local spectrum, and prove that this map preserves a nonzero rational degree-$-3$ class. Locality then forces the map to factor through the canonical localization unit. The class is detected in degree-three stabilizer cohomology; the main argument compares its height-three and height-two realizations.

Fix $p\geq5$, put $k=\mathbb F_{p^6}$, and choose $C=\mathbb C_p$ with compatible constant-field data. For $n=2,3$, let $\Gamma_n$ be the height-$n$ Honda formal group over $k$, and put $$E_n=E(k,\Gamma_n),\qquad
 P_n=\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}_k(\Gamma_n)=\mathcal O_{D_n}^{\times}.$$ Here $D_n$ is the division algebra over $\mathbb Q_p$ of invariant $1/n$, and $P_n$ is the nonextended stabilizer. Set $$G=P_3\times P_2,\qquad
 \Delta=\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(k/\mathbb F_p),\qquad G_3=P_3\rtimes\Delta.$$ The field $k$ contains the endomorphism fields of both Honda groups.

On the height-exactly-two stratum $k((u_2))$ of the height-three deformation space, the Gross–Torii extension identifies the connected height-two group with $\Gamma_2$. Its valued-field completion $\widehat L$ carries the two framing actions, hence an action of $G$. The solid Lubin–Tate construction of Barthel–Mann–Ray–Schlank–Senger–Weinstein–Zhou (Barthel et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BMR2026](#ref-BMR2026)] gives the connected completed theory $$\widehat B^\square=E^\square(\widehat L,(\Gamma_2)_{\widehat L}),
 \qquad \mathcal D=\bigl((\widehat B^\square)^{hG}\bigr)(*).$$ The superscript $\square$ records the solid structure and continuous action; $(*)$ is evaluation in ordinary spectra. Fixed points are formed before this evaluation.

<a id="eq:overview-detector"></a>
Section <a href="#sec:completed-tower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completed-tower">3</a> constructs equivariant maps $E_3^\square\to\widehat B^\square\leftarrow E_2^\square$. The first requires a section on oriented deformations, obtained by adjoining the marked étale factor to the connected deformation. The second is the canonical connected base-change map. Restriction from $G_3$ to $P_3$, inflation along $G\to P_3$, and the first map define $g:L_{K(3)}S\to\mathcal D$. Every profinite evaluation of $\widehat B^\square$ is $K(2)$-local, so its bar totalization $\mathcal D$ is too. Thus $$\label{eq:overview-detector}
 L_{K(3)}S\xrightarrow{\eta}L_{K(2)}L_{K(3)}S
       \xrightarrow{\overline g}\mathcal D,
 \qquad g=\overline g\eta.$$ The source identification and these actual maps are established in [prop:completed-theory,lem:local-detector] [prop:completed-theory](#prop:completed-theory), [lem:local-detector](#lem:local-detector).

Write $N_3:P_3\to\mathbb Z_p^\times$ for reduced norm and $P_3^1=N_3^{-1}(\mu_{p-1})$. The completed comparison $$E_2^\square\xrightarrow{\sim}(\widehat B^\square)^{hP_3^1}$$ reduces the detector’s coefficients to height two. We prove the individual-coefficient comparison as well as this instance of (Barthel et al. 2026, Theorem 5.2.6) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[BMR2026](#ref-BMR2026)]. The corresponding equivalence for the uncompleted half sphere remains a separate conjecture (Barthel et al. 2026, Conjectures 1.1.1 and 5.1.13) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BMR2026](#ref-BMR2026)], with a conditional coheight-one consequence in (Barthel et al. 2026, Proposition 5.1.17) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BMR2026](#ref-BMR2026)]. The completed target in <a href="#eq:overview-detector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:overview-detector">[eq:overview-detector]</a> suffices here.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Transporting the primitive

The starting classes are nonzero elements $e_{P_n}\in H^3_{\mathrm{cts}}(P_n,\mathbb Q_p)$, for $n=2,3$. Under rational Lie comparison they are represented, up to nonzero scalars, by $$(U,V,W)%
  \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=27FC}%
  \longmapsto\EndAccSupp{}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{\mathrm{red}}\bigl(U[V,W]\bigr).$$ This is the classical cocycle attached to an invariant bilinear form (Chevalley and Eilenberg 1948, sec. 21) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[ChevalleyEilenberg1948](#ref-ChevalleyEilenberg1948)] <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Koszul1950](#ref-Koszul1950)], (Koszul 1950, sec. 11). Section <a href="#sec:cohomology" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cohomology">2</a> constructs these classes and their linear counterparts $e_{J_n}$ for $$J_n=\{g\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_n(\mathbb Q_p):|\det g|_p=1\}.$$ The trace class restricts nontrivially from rank three to rank two; the same compatibility holds for the relevant parabolic subgroup, with determinant lattices retained. The low-rank calculation and the building argument are given in <a href="#app:background" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:background">9</a>.

To compare division-algebra and linear classes, we use a line modification of a form of the rank-$n$, degree-one Fargues–Fontaine bundle $\mathcal E_n^0=\mathcal O(1/n)$, retaining a determinant lattice. The stack $\mathcal M_n$ of such modifications has two presentations: $$\mathcal M_n\simeq[\mathbb P_C^{n-1,\diamondsuit}/P_n]
       \simeq[\Omega_C^{n-1,\diamondsuit}/J_n].$$ Here projective space parametrizes quotient lines of the untilt fiber, and Drinfeld space $\Omega_C^{n-1}$ is the complement of the $\mathbb Q_p$-rational hyperplanes. The relative bundle correspondence and basic-stratum theorems of Fargues–Scholze provide the torsors in families (Fargues and Scholze 2024, Theorem II.2.19, Corollary II.2.20, and Theorems III.2.4 and III.4.5) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[FS](#ref-FS)]. Keeping a lattice in the determinant local system gives the structure group $J_n$. These presentations follow Faltings’s Lubin–Tate/Drinfeld duality (Faltings 2002, Introduction) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Faltings2002](#ref-Faltings2002)], in the perfectoid form of Scholze–Weinstein with its exchanged period maps (Scholze and Weinstein 2013, Theorem 7.2.3) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[SW2013](#ref-SW2013)]. Section <a href="#sec:modifications" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:modifications">4</a> constructs the determinant-lattice comparison used here.

For the geometric cohomology below, write $H^i_{\mathrm b}$ for integral cohomology followed by inversion of $p$. Integral cohomology is the derived inverse limit of finite $p$-power coefficient complexes, as specified in Section <a href="#sec:cohomology" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cohomology">2</a>. The two presentations have complementary roles. The projective bundle formula singles out a one-dimensional Galois-invariant line in $H^3_{\mathrm b}(\mathcal M_n)$, containing the nonzero image of $e_{P_n}$. Using the Galois weights in the integral Drinfeld cohomology theorem of Colmez–Dospinescu–Nizioł (Colmez, Dospinescu, and Nizioł 2021, Theorems 1.1 and 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[CDN2021](#ref-CDN2021)], we prove that the image of $e_{J_n}$ is also nonzero and belongs to that line. Thus the two classes are proportional.

The completed tower carries a surjection from the rank-three bundle to the rank-two bundle. A common line modification turns this into an exact sequence of rational local systems of ranks $1,3,2$. Parabolic restriction and projective-bundle injectivity then give $$e_{P_3}|_{Y_C}=a\,e_{P_2}|_{Y_C},\qquad a\in\mathbb Q_p^\times,
 \quad
 Y_C=[\mathop{\mathrm{Spd}}\widehat L/G]\times_{\mathop{\mathrm{Spd}}k}\mathop{\mathrm{Spd}}C.$$ Section <a href="#sec:degree-three" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:degree-three">5</a> proves this relation. Both classes on $Y_C$ could still vanish, so nonvanishing requires a further step.

Section <a href="#sec:witt-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:witt-transfer">6</a> first reflects the relation from $Y_C$ to $Y=[\mathop{\mathrm{Spd}}\widehat L/G]$ with Witt coefficients. The maps from constants become equivalences after $P_3^1$-descent; a bounded finite free complex proves injectivity under the change of constants $W(k)\to W(C^\flat)$. The continuous additive retraction onto Witt constants of Barthel–Schlank–Stapleton–Weinstein (Barthel et al. 2025, Proposition 2.5.1 and Corollary 2.5.9) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[BSSW2025](#ref-BSSW2025)] then proves nonvanishing of the height-two class in deformation coefficients. If $c_n$ is the image of $e_{P_n}$ under projection from $G$ and the coefficient unit, the result is $$c_3=a\,c_2\ne0
 \quad\text{in }H^3(G,\pi_0\widehat B^\square)[1/p].$$

Finally, Section <a href="#sec:detection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:detection">7</a> uses the descent spectral sequences for the actual map $g$. A finite cohomological bound produces a finite integral filtration in each ordinary homotopy degree. After rationalization, central scalar automorphisms kill all coefficient degrees $t\ne0$. With $s$ denoting group-cohomological degree, the nonzero coefficient map in bidegree $(s,t)=(3,0)$ therefore survives to degree $-3$ ordinary homotopy. The factorization <a href="#eq:overview-detector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:overview-detector">[eq:overview-detector]</a> proves the main theorem, and Section <a href="#sec:wedge-obstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:wedge-obstruction">8</a> gives its wedge consequence.

Continuous invariants throughout the proof retain their solid coefficient objects, and inversion of $p$ follows integral cohomology. This order matters for the noncompact spaces and groups that occur here.

<a id="sec:cohomology"></a>
### Integral cohomology and degree-three group classes

Our goal is to construct the degree-three classes that will be transported through the two-height tower, together with the exact restriction maps between their linear realizations. We first fix the integral coefficient convention and prove the finite-resolution statement that will also control the later Galois-weight and ordinary-descent arguments.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Coefficients and continuous descent

<a id="eq:integral-group-cochains"></a>
Put $\Lambda_m=\mathbb Z/p^m$. For a small v-stack $X$ occurring below, put $$R\Gamma_{\mathrm{int}}(X)
   =R\!\varprojlim_m R\Gamma_v(X,\Lambda_m),\qquad
 H^i_{\mathrm{int}}(X)=H^iR\Gamma_{\mathrm{int}}(X),\qquad
 H^i_{\mathrm b}(X)=H^i_{\mathrm{int}}(X)[1/p].$$ For a locally profinite group $G$, define separately in $D(\mathrm{Ab})$ $$\label{eq:integral-group-cochains}
\begin{gathered}
 R\Gamma_{\mathrm{int}}(G)=R\!\varprojlim_m C_{\mathrm{cts}}^{\bullet}(G,\Lambda_m),
 \\
 H^i_{\mathrm{int}}(G)=H^iR\Gamma_{\mathrm{int}}(G),
 \qquad H^i_{\mathrm b}(G)=H^i_{\mathrm{int}}(G)[1/p].
\end{gathered}$$ Here $C_{\mathrm{cts}}^{\bullet}$ is the inhomogeneous continuous cochain complex, with trivial discrete coefficients. The standard resolution in the condensed classifying topos $BG_{\mathrm{pro\acute et}}$, the topos of condensed sets with $G$-action, gives the natural comparison $$C_{\mathrm{cts}}^{\bullet}(G,\Lambda_m)\simeq
 \mathop{\mathrm{RHom}}_{\Lambda_m[\underline G]\text{-}\mathrm{Cond}}
       (\underline{\Lambda_m},\underline{\Lambda_m}).$$ This is the external derived Hom; its continuous-cochain calculation for these solid topological coefficients is the standard-resolution proof in (Anschütz 2020, sec. 2, pp. 5–7, Lemmas 2.1–2.2) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Anschutz2020](#ref-Anschutz2020)]. We next construct the maps from this group complex to geometric quotients.

<a id="eq:group-cochain-bridge"></a>
For a condensed $G$-complex $K$, write $R\Gamma(G,K)$ for its *internal* derived condensed invariants, and $R\Gamma(G,K)(*)$ for their point value, which is the external derived Hom (Anschütz 2020, sec. 2, p. 8) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Anschutz2020](#ref-Anschutz2020)]. Topological coefficients in this notation are implicitly condensed. Reduction on finite cochains is degreewise surjective: a function lifts by composing with a section of the finite coefficient reduction. Compatible finite continuous functions are exactly continuous $\mathbb Z_p$-valued functions. Hence $$\label{eq:group-cochain-bridge}
 R\Gamma_{\mathrm{int}}(G)\simeq C_{\mathrm{cts}}^{\bullet}(G,\mathbb Z_p)
       \simeq R\Gamma(G,\underline{\mathbb Z_p})(*).$$ For the last comparison, $\mathbb Z_p$ with its $p$-adic topology is solid by (Anschütz 2020, sec. 1, pp. 4–5) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Anschutz2020](#ref-Anschutz2020)], so the same standard-resolution proof applies. We do not identify the internal invariant object with the discrete condensation of the external cochain complex for a noncompact group; see (Anschütz 2020, Lemma 2.5 and Remark 2.6) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Anschutz2020](#ref-Anschutz2020)]. The comparisons in <a href="#eq:group-cochain-bridge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:group-cochain-bridge">[eq:group-cochain-bridge]</a> are natural for continuous group homomorphisms. For compact $G$, continuous functions $G^r\to\mathbb Q_p$ are bounded, so $$C_{\mathrm{cts}}(G^r,\mathbb Z_p)[1/p]=C_{\mathrm{cts}}(G^r,\mathbb Q_p).$$ Consequently $H^*_{\mathrm b}(G)$ agrees with ordinary continuous $\mathbb Q_p$-cohomology for compact $G$. We use this last identification only in that case.

Tate twists are taken at each finite level before taking the derived limit. All maps of coefficients and all descent maps are formed integrally. In particular, $H^i_{\mathrm b}(X)$ is not defined as $H^i_v(X,\widehat\mathbb Z_p[1/p])$. Moving inversion of $p$ before cohomology can change the result for a noncompact space or group.

For an analytic adic space in this paper, its étale site agrees with the étale site of its diamond (Scholze 2026, Lemma 15.6) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[ScholzeDiamonds](#ref-ScholzeDiamonds)]. On a locally spatial diamond, bounded-below étale complexes have the same derived cohomology on the diamond étale, quasi-pro-étale, and v-sites (Scholze 2026, Proposition 14.10) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[ScholzeDiamonds](#ref-ScholzeDiamonds)]. We apply these statements to finite constant coefficients, one level at a time. The integral analytic pro-étale comparison used for Drinfeld space is the separate comparison in (Colmez, Dospinescu, and Nizioł 2021, sec. 5) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[CDN2021](#ref-CDN2021)]. Quotient stacks are handled by descent for their covers. Only after the finite-level comparisons do we take $R\!\varprojlim_m$.

<a id="eq:finite-constant-cochains"></a>
Here is the map from group classes to geometric quotients. For a small v-stack base $B$, put $B_BG=[B/\underline G]$, where $G$ acts trivially on $B$. A $G$-space $X$ over $B$ gives a map $[X/G]\to B_BG$; a $G$-torsor $T\to Z$ over $B$ gives its classifying map $Z\to B_BG$. At each finite level define $$\label{eq:finite-constant-cochains}
\begin{split}
 u_{X,G,m}:C_{\mathrm{cts}}^{\bullet}(G,\Lambda_m)
 &\longrightarrow
   \operatorname{Tot}_{a}R\Gamma_v
       (X\times\underline{G^a},\Lambda_m)\\
 &\simeq R\Gamma_v([X/G],\Lambda_m).
\end{split}$$ In degree $a$, pull a locally constant function on $G^a$ back along the projection from $X\times\underline{G^a}$.
